I just… I don’t really like anyone seeing how much I can put away. It’s embarrassing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Um… I guess I’m here because I’ve been having some trouble with eating at night. It’s probably not as bad as it sounds, but once I start picking at food, I tell myself, “Okay, that’s enough, Santiago,” and then… my hand just keeps going back. Like I don’t really get to make the decision anymore. It’s been worrying me a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're eating at night?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really, no. I’d like to say I’m in control, and maybe at first I am, but once I start picking at food late at night, I’ll tell myself, “That’s enough, Santiago,” and then my hand just kind of keeps going back. Like I don’t fully get to make the decision anymore, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after eating at night?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it guilt exactly… maybe more like I feel kind of disappointed in myself, like, “Why did I do that again?” But I try not to make a huge deal out of it. I mean, I know people snack at night, right? So… yeah, maybe a little, but I don’t know if guilt is the right word.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself eating at night like this?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess it’s been going on for the past several months. Not every single night or anything, but most weeks it happens at least once. Usually it’s on a night when there are a lot of leftovers around and everyone else has gone to bed, so… I end up back in the kitchen. I don’t know if that sounds like a lot.\n\nDoctor: When you're eating at night like this, do you usually eat a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, sometimes it probably is more than I’d want to admit. Not every time, but some nights after work, if there’s food there, I’ll end up going through, like, a few tamales, a big bowl of pozole, tortillas… and then maybe whatever rice or beans are left.\n\nI kind of tell myself I’m just cleaning up the leftovers or putting things away, but… yeah, I guess that is a large amount, isn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these nighttime episodes?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, sometimes it feels that way. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m totally out of control, but once I start picking at the food late at night, I’ll tell myself, “Okay, that’s enough, Santiago,” and then my hand just keeps going back. Like I already decided to stop, but somehow I’m still eating. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating much more quickly than usual during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, not like I’m timing myself or anything, but it’s definitely faster than if I’m sitting down with my family for a meal.\n\nIt’s more like I’m standing there and just trying to get through it before anybody wakes up or comes in, so I’m not really tasting it much. I know that probably doesn’t sound great… but yeah, I do eat it pretty fast sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're aware of what you're doing the entire time you're eating at night?\n\nPatient: I think I’m aware… mostly. Like, I know I’m in the kitchen and I know I’m eating. It’s not like I black out or anything.\n\nBut I guess I’m not really paying attention the way I should be. It’s more automatic, like I’m on autopilot a little. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually try to hide the fact that you're eating at night from your family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t make a big show of it or anything, but yes, I do try to keep it quiet.\n\nI usually wait until my aunt and everyone else in the house is asleep before I go into the kitchen. I just… I don’t really like anyone seeing how much I can put away. It’s embarrassing. Maybe embarrassed, yeah.\n\nAfterward I usually feel kind of stupid about it, like, “Why did I do that?” But I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I’ve started kind of avoiding hanging around the kitchen late with my family, because I feel uneasy around the food and I don’t want anyone noticing that. I don’t know if that counts as a real problem, but it does make me feel a little worse about myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating at night is causing you a lot of stress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: A lot of stress or anxiety? Like I don’t really get to make the decision anymore. It’s been worrying me a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're eating at night?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really, no. I’d like to say I’m in control, and maybe at first I am, but once I start picking at food late at night, I’ll tell myself, “That’s enough, Santiago,” and then my hand just kind of keeps going back. Like I don’t fully get to make the decision anymore, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after eating at night?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it guilt exactly… maybe more like I feel kind of disappointed in myself, like, “Why did I do that again?” But I try not to make a huge deal out of it. I mean, I know people snack at night, right? So… yeah, maybe a little, but I don’t know if guilt is the right word.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself eating at night like this?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess it’s been going on for the past several months. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m totally out of control, but once I start picking at the food late at night, I’ll tell myself, “Okay, that’s enough, Santiago,” and then my hand just keeps going back. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating much more quickly than usual during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I guess… not exactly like I don’t think at all, but in the moment I’m not really thinking it through the way I should.

Like I don’t really get to make the decision anymore. It’s been worrying me a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're eating at night?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really, no. I’d like to say I’m in control, and maybe at first I am, but once I start picking at food late at night, I’ll tell myself, “That’s enough, Santiago,” and then my hand just kind of keeps going back. Like I don’t fully get to make the decision anymore, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after eating at night?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it guilt exactly… maybe more like I feel kind of disappointed in myself, like, “Why did I do that again?” But I try not to make a huge deal out of it. I mean, I know people snack at night, right? So… yeah, maybe a little, but I don’t know if guilt is the right word.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself eating at night like this?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess it’s been going on for the past several months. Not every single night or anything, but most weeks it happens at least once. Usually it’s on a night when there are a lot of leftovers around and everyone else has gone to bed, so… I end up back in the kitchen. I don’t know if that sounds like a lot.\n\nDoctor: When you're eating at night like this, do you usually eat a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, sometimes it probably is more than I’d want to admit. Not every time, but some nights after work, if there’s food there, I’ll end up going through, like, a few tamales, a big bowl of pozole, tortillas… and then maybe whatever rice or beans are left.\n\nI kind of tell myself I’m just cleaning up the leftovers or putting things away, but… yeah, I guess that is a large amount, isn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these nighttime episodes?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, sometimes it feels that way. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m totally out of control, but once I start picking at the food late at night, I’ll tell myself, “Okay, that’s enough, Santiago,” and then my hand just keeps going back. Like I already decided to stop, but somehow I’m still eating. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating much more quickly than usual during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, not like I’m timing myself or anything, but it’s definitely faster than if I’m sitting down with my family for a meal.\n\nIt’s more like I’m standing there and just trying to get through it before anybody wakes up or comes in, so I’m not really tasting it much. I know that probably doesn’t sound great… but yeah, I do eat it pretty fast sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're aware of what you're doing the entire time you're eating at night?\n\nPatient: I think I’m aware… mostly. Like, I know I’m in the kitchen and I know I’m eating. It’s not like I black out or anything.\n\nBut I guess I’m not really paying attention the way I should be. It’s more automatic, like I’m on autopilot a little. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually try to hide the fact that you're eating at night from your family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t make a big show of it or anything, but yes, I do try to keep it quiet.\n\nI usually wait until my aunt and everyone else in the house is asleep before I go into the kitchen. I just… I don’t really like anyone seeing how much I can put away. It’s embarrassing. I know that probably sounds bad, but I’d rather no one notice, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel ashamed of yourself after these nighttime eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I’d call it ashamed exactly. I’ve started kind of avoiding hanging around the kitchen late with my family, because I feel uneasy around the food and I don’t want anyone noticing that. I don’t know if that counts as a real problem, but it does make me feel a little worse about myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating at night is causing you a lot of stress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: A lot of stress or anxiety? I don’t know if I’d say a lot, no.\n\nIt’s more like… I feel uneasy about it, and I wish I didn’t do it, but I’m not panicking over it or anything. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these episodes because you can't control your amount of food intake or because the food is just too available?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s more that I don’t feel totally in control once I start. I’m… partly aware, yeah. I don’t usually count it out or anything, so I could tell myself it’s not that much. But if I’m being honest, some nights after work I’ll go through several tamales, a big bowl of pozole, tortillas, and then whatever rice or beans are left.

I mean, I know people snack at night, right? I don’t want to make it sound like I’m totally out of control, but once I start picking at the food late at night, I’ll tell myself, “Okay, that’s enough, Santiago,” and then my hand just keeps going back. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating much more quickly than usual during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I know that probably doesn’t sound great… but yeah, I do eat it pretty fast sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're aware of what you're doing the entire time you're eating at night?\n\nPatient: I think I’m aware… mostly. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually try to hide the fact that you're eating at night from your family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t make a big show of it or anything, but yes, I do try to keep it quiet.\n\nI usually wait until my aunt and everyone else in the house is asleep before I go into the kitchen. I just… I don’t really like anyone seeing how much I can put away. It’s embarrassing. It’s probably not as bad as it sounds, but once I start picking at food, I tell myself, “Okay, that’s enough, Santiago,” and then… my hand just keeps going back. Like I don’t really get to make the decision anymore. It’s been worrying me a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're eating at night?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really, no. I’d like to say I’m in control, and maybe at first I am, but once I start picking at food late at night, I’ll tell myself, “That’s enough, Santiago,” and then my hand just kind of keeps going back. Like I don’t fully get to make the decision anymore, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after eating at night?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it guilt exactly… maybe more like I feel kind of disappointed in myself, like, “Why did I do that again?” But I try not to make a huge deal out of it. I mean, I know people snack at night, right? So… yeah, maybe a little, but I don’t know if guilt is the right word.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself eating at night like this?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess it’s been going on for the past several months. Not every single night or anything, but most weeks it happens at least once. Usually it’s on a night when there are a lot of leftovers around and everyone else has gone to bed, so… I end up back in the kitchen. I don’t know if that sounds like a lot.\n\nDoctor: When you're eating at night like this, do you usually eat a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, sometimes it probably is more than I’d want to admit. Not every time, but some nights after work, if there’s food there, I’ll end up going through, like, a few tamales, a big bowl of pozole, tortillas… and then maybe whatever rice or beans are left.\n\nI kind of tell myself I’m just cleaning up the leftovers or putting things away, but… yeah, I guess that is a large amount, isn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these nighttime episodes?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, sometimes it feels that way. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m totally out of control, but once I start picking at the food late at night, I’ll tell myself, “Okay, that’s enough, Santiago,” and then my hand just keeps going back. Like I already decided to stop, but somehow I’m still eating. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating much more quickly than usual during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, not like I’m timing myself or anything, but it’s definitely faster than if I’m sitting down with my family for a meal.\n\nIt’s more like I’m standing there and just trying to get through it before anybody wakes up or comes in, so I’m not really tasting it much. I know that probably doesn’t sound great… but yeah, I do eat it pretty fast sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're aware of what you're doing the entire time you're eating at night?\n\nPatient: I think I’m aware… mostly. Like, I know I’m in the kitchen and I know I’m eating. It’s not like I black out or anything.\n\nBut I guess I’m not really paying attention the way I should be. It’s more automatic, like I’m on autopilot a little. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually try to hide the fact that you're eating at night from your family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t make a big show of it or anything, but yes, I do try to keep it quiet.\n\nI usually wait until my aunt and everyone else in the house is asleep before I go into the kitchen. I just… I don’t really like anyone seeing how much I can put away. It’s embarrassing. I know that probably sounds bad, but I’d rather no one notice, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel ashamed of yourself after these nighttime eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I’d call it ashamed exactly. Maybe embarrassed, yeah.\n\nAfterward I usually feel kind of stupid about it, like, “Why did I do that?” But I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic.\n\nBut, yeah, there are times where once I start picking at food late at night, I’ll tell myself, “That’s enough, Santiago,” and then my hand just keeps going back anyway. I mean, I’m still getting to my shipping and receiving shifts, and I’m handling what I need to handle.\n\nBut… yeah, I guess it does affect me some. I’ve started kind of avoiding hanging around the kitchen late with my family, because I feel uneasy around the food and I don’t want anyone noticing that. I don’t know if that counts as a real problem, but it does make me feel a little worse about myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating at night is causing you a lot of stress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: A lot of stress or anxiety? I don’t know if I’d say a lot, no.\n\nIt’s more like… I feel uneasy about it, and I wish I didn’t do it, but I’m not panicking over it or anything. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. It bothers me some, sure, but I’m still getting through my day. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these episodes because you can't control your amount of food intake or because the food is just too available?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s more that I don’t feel totally in control once I start. Like I already decided to stop, but I don’t… quite follow through. I don’t know if that’s what you mean by unable to stop, but yeah, it feels a little like I’m not the one making the decision in that moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're completely losing control of your eating during these nighttime episodes?\n\nPatient: Completely losing control… I don’t know if I’d put it that strongly. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic.\n\nBut, yeah, there are times where once I start picking at food late at night, I’ll tell myself, “That’s enough, Santiago,” and then my hand just keeps going back anyway. It kind of feels like I don’t get to make the decision anymore, at least in that moment. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating during these episodes without thinking about the consequences of what you're doing?\n\nPatient: I guess… not exactly like I don’t think at all, but in the moment I’m not really thinking it through the way I should.\n\nWhat worries me is once I start picking at the food late at night, I’ll tell myself, “That’s enough, Santiago,” and then my hand keeps going back anyway. It’s like I know I should stop, but I don’t quite get to make that decision right then. I don’t know if that counts as not thinking about the consequences, but… it kind of feels that way sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a sense of pleasure or enjoyment while eating at night during these episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. Like I already decided to stop, but I don’t… quite follow through. I don’t know if that’s what you mean by unable to stop, but yeah, it feels a little like I’m not the one making the decision in that moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're completely losing control of your eating during these nighttime episodes?\n\nPatient: Completely losing control… I don’t know if I’d put it that strongly. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic.\n\nBut, yeah, there are times where once I start picking at food late at night, I’ll tell myself, “That’s enough, Santiago,” and then my hand just keeps going back anyway. It kind of feels like I don’t get to make the decision anymore, at least in that moment. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating during these episodes without thinking about the consequences of what you're doing?\n\nPatient: I guess… not exactly like I don’t think at all, but in the moment I’m not really thinking it through the way I should.\n\nWhat worries me is once I start picking at the food late at night, I’ll tell myself, “That’s enough, Santiago,” and then my hand keeps going back anyway. It’s like I know I should stop, but I don’t quite get to make that decision right then. I don’t know if that counts as not thinking about the consequences, but… it kind of feels that way sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a sense of pleasure or enjoyment while eating at night during these episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I wouldn’t say it’s like… fun or anything. Maybe there’s a little comfort in the moment, just because I’m doing something familiar, but I haven’t really noticed it as pleasure exactly.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you’re asking. It’s more like I’m just eating, and then afterward I’m thinking, “Why did I do that?”\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these nighttime eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I mean… guilty might be a strong word. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nI do feel kind of bad afterward, like disappointed in myself, I guess. I’ll think, “Why couldn’t I just stop?” But I usually try not to dwell on it too much. Is that the kind of guilt you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are you aware of how much food you're eating during these nighttime episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m… partly aware, yeah. I don’t usually count it out or anything, so I could tell myself it’s not that much. But if I’m being honest, some nights after work I’ll go through several tamales, a big bowl of pozole, tortillas, and then whatever rice or beans are left.\n\nIt sounds worse saying it out loud. At the time I’m standing there in the kitchen like I’m just cleaning up leftovers, so I don’t fully register the amount until after. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you eat at night because you're embarrassed to eat in front of others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not all the time, but I guess that is part of it.\n\nI don’t really like anyone seeing how much I can eat, so I’ll kind of wait until my aunt and everybody else in the house is asleep. Then I try to be quiet in the kitchen, like I’m just getting a little something. I know that probably sounds bad… I just don’t want people looking at me or saying something.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat at night in secret because you're embarrassed about how much food you're consuming?\n\nPatient: Yeah… usually, I guess. I don’t love saying it like that, but that’s pretty much what happens.\n\nI don’t like anyone seeing how much I can put away, so I’ll wait until my aunt and the rest of the house are asleep. Then I try to keep it quiet in the kitchen, like I’m not making a big deal out of it. It’s embarrassing, honestly."
